Well, um... I really try not to let it affect things too much! I'm still doing great at my job, and I manage everything at home just fine. I promise I'm not falling apart or anything like that.

Well, um... it really doesn't last very long at all, I promise! I'm usually pretty quick to bounce back.

It's just... when it comes to getting on an airplane, that's where I get a little, um, stuck. I hope that makes sense? I know it probably sounds a bit strange to just have this one specific thing that bothers me so much.\n\nDoctor: When you think about flying, does it get to the point where you actively avoid it, like turning down trips or opportunities to travel?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I *actively* avoid it, exactly. I mean, I still try to be reasonable about things. But... lately, I have found myself, you know, suggesting we just drive instead of fly for vacations, even if it takes a lot longer. And there was a work training recently that I... well, I kind of found an excuse not to go to, just because the flight felt a bit too overwhelming right now. \n\nAnd, honestly, even just sitting down to plan a trip or talk about booking tickets... I get so worked up that I usually have to ask my husband to sit right there with me, just so I don't completely lose my nerve and cancel the whole idea. I know that probably sounds incredibly needy of me, relying on him just to talk about a trip. I'm really trying to be better about it, though! Is it... is it normal to need that kind of support for something like this?\n\nDoctor: When you think about flying or start planning a flight, do you ever feel sudden, intense physical symptoms like your heart racing, feeling dizzy, short of breath, or like you're losing control?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I completely lose control or anything dramatic like that. I really try to keep it together! But... I guess I do get a little bit of a physical reaction, yeah. \n\nLike, if I'm just looking at flights on my laptop for a work conference, or... honestly, even if I'm just outside and hear a plane flying overhead, my chest does get a little tight right away. And I get this... this sudden rush of panic, I suppose you could call it. It's just this strong feeling like I need to immediately shut my laptop or just get away from whatever is making me think about it. \n\nBut I always try to calm myself down right after! I know it's silly to let a sound or a website make my chest tight. Does... does that sound like the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these physical reactions and feelings of panic happen mostly when you're actually thinking about an upcoming flight, or do they also happen in other situations when you're not directly anticipating flying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, it's really just tied to the flying. I mean, I don't just sit around having panic attacks for no reason! I'm actually a very calm person normally, I swear. \n\nIt's really only when I'm, you know, faced with something related to airplanes. Like if I have to book a ticket, or if a trip is coming up and I start thinking about being on the plane... or like I said, if one flies right over me and catches me off guard. If I'm not dealing with anything flight-related, I'm completely fine. I don't have those chest-tightening moments at the grocery store or anything like that. \n\nI hope that's a good thing? Like, it means I'm mostly okay, right?\n\nDoctor: Has this fear of flying been present for a long time, or did it start more recently after that honeymoon flight you mentioned?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, it hasn't been a lifelong thing or anything like that. I used to be completely fine with airplanes! I promise I wasn't always this much of a worrywart about it. \n\nIt really just started right after that turbulent flight back from my honeymoon. That was... gosh, about two years and five months ago now. I know it probably sounds a bit silly that I'm still letting one bumpy ride affect me over two years later. I really am trying to get past it, I just haven't quite figured out how yet. \n\nIs it... is it okay that it's lasted that long from just one bad experience? I hope I don't sound completely ridiculous holding onto it for two and a half years.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of flying caused you any significant distress or affected your ability to function in your job, relationships, or other important areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to let it affect things too much! I'm still doing great at my job, and I manage everything at home just fine. I promise I'm not falling apart or anything like that. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, it has gotten in the way a little bit. Nothing major! Just... well, my supervisor has suggested a few trainings and conferences that would be really good for my career, and I've... I've sort of been finding excuses to pass on them because they involve flying. I know I shouldn't do that, and I always feel a bit guilty about it. \n\nAnd, um, on a personal level... I've been really excited to go to Florence for a long time, but I keep pushing the trip back because I just can't bring myself to book the flight. \n\nSo I guess it is causing a little bit of trouble, but I'm really trying to work on it! I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not taking my career or my life seriously. Does... does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long does the anxiety or discomfort typically last once you've been exposed to something related to flying, like booking a flight or hearing a plane overhead?\n\nPatient: Well, um... it really doesn't last very long at all, I promise! I'm usually pretty quick to bounce back. \n\nIf I hear a plane or see one, you know, it's just a quick flutter in my chest, and then I tell myself it's fine and I move on. It might take a few minutes for my heart to settle completely, but I don't let it ruin my whole day or anything. And with booking... well, I just get a little worked up while I'm thinking about it, but once I step away from the computer, I'm usually back to normal pretty quickly. \n\nI really try not to dwell on it, you know? I just take a deep breath and keep going. That's a good way to handle it, right? I hope I'm not making a bigger deal out of it than I should be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden surge of panic or physical symptoms like chest tightness when you weren't thinking about flying or being near an airplane?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, definitely not! I promise I don't just panic out of nowhere. \n\nIf I'm just going about my day, you know, working at the preschool or running errands, I'm completely fine. I don't get those chest tightness feelings or anything like that unless it's specifically about... well, about airplanes. \n\nI'm actually a very calm person normally! I really don't want you to think I'm just anxious all the time about everything. It's really just this one little thing with the flying. That's... that's normal for this kind of thing, right? To just have it be about the one specific fear?\n\nDoctor: When you experience these physical symptoms like chest tightness or a rush of panic related to flying, do they ever peak within a few minutes and feel like a full panic attack, even if short-lived?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, a full panic attack? I promise I'm not the type to just sit around stressing for days! If I know I have a trip coming up, I just focus on my work with the kids at the preschool and keep myself busy. I'm pretty good at just putting it out of my mind, you know? \n\nIt really only hits me when I'm actually right there, like walking into the airport or getting ready to board. Um, well, I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it bother me, but if I'm, say, looking up flights for a work conference or something, or even if I just hear a loud plane flying right overhead, I do get this... this quick little tightness in my chest.
